## Specification (func.py as given)
```python
def min_tile_moves(K: int, S_x: int, S_y: int, T_x: int, T_y: int) -> int:
    """ Find the minimum number of moves between tiles on a coordinate plane.
    
    Tiles are placed according to these rules:
    - For integer triple (i, j, k) with 0 <= k < K:
      - If i and j have same parity: tile covers iK <= x <= (i+1)K and jK+k <= y <= jK+k+1
      - If i and j have different parity: tile covers iK+k <= x <= iK+k+1 and jK <= y <= (j+1)K
    
    Two tiles are adjacent if their edges share a positive-length segment.
    
    Args:
        K: The tile parameter (2 <= K <= 10^16)
        S_x, S_y: Starting coordinates (tile containing (S_x + 0.5, S_y + 0.5))
        T_x, T_y: Target coordinates (tile containing (T_x + 0.5, T_y + 0.5))
    
    Returns:
        Minimum number of moves to adjacent tiles needed to go from start to target tile.
    
    >>> min_tile_moves(3, -2, 1, 4, -1)
    4
    >>> min_tile_moves(4, 8, 8, 0, 2)
    4
    >>> min_tile_moves(5, -1000000000000, -1000000000000, 1000000000000, 1000000000000)
    800000000000
    """
```

## Final func.py
```python
def min_tile_moves(K: int, S_x: int, S_y: int, T_x: int, T_y: int) -> int:
    """ Find the minimum number of moves between tiles on a coordinate plane.
    
    Tiles are placed according to these rules:
    - For integer triple (i, j, k) with 0 <= k < K:
      - If i and j have same parity: tile covers iK <= x <= (i+1)K and jK+k <= y <= jK+k+1
      - If i and j have different parity: tile covers iK+k <= x <= iK+k+1 and jK <= y <= (j+1)K
    
    Two tiles are adjacent if their edges share a positive-length segment.
    
    Args:
        K: The tile parameter (2 <= K <= 10^16)
        S_x, S_y: Starting coordinates (tile containing (S_x + 0.5, S_y + 0.5))
        T_x, T_y: Target coordinates (tile containing (T_x + 0.5, T_y + 0.5))
    
    Returns:
        Minimum number of moves to adjacent tiles needed to go from start to target tile.
    
    >>> min_tile_moves(3, -2, 1, 4, -1)
    4
    >>> min_tile_moves(4, 8, 8, 0, 2)
    4
    >>> min_tile_moves(5, -1000000000000, -1000000000000, 1000000000000, 1000000000000)
    800000000000
    """
    raise NotImplementedError
```

Let me think about the adjacency more carefully.

Consider moving from cell (i,j) to (i+1,j). 
- If (i,j) is H (same parity) and (i+1,j) is V (different parity):
  - H(i,j,k) -> V(i+1,j,0) costs 1. But you can also go to V(i+1,j,0) from any k in H.
  - Also, V(i+1,j,0) -> H(i,j,k) for any k.
  - So the cost to go from H cell to V cell is 1, but you land at k=0 in V. If you want to go to a different k in V, you pay extra.

- If (i,j) is V and (i+1,j) is H:
  - V(i,j,K-1) -> H(i+1,j,k) for any k. So from V you need to be at k=K-1 to exit right. Or you can pay to get to k=K-1.
  - Similarly, H(i+1,j,k) -> V(i,j,K-1) for all k.

So crossing from V to H (moving right) requires being at k=K-1 in V, but you can land at any k in H.
Crossing from H to V (moving right) can be done from any k in H, but you land at k=0 in V.

Similarly, for vertical moves:
- H to V (up): H(i,j,K-1) -> V(i,j+1,k) for any k. Need k=K-1 in H.
- V to H (up): V(i,j,k) -> H(i,j+1,0) for any k. Land at k=0 in H.

Wait, let me double-check:
- H(i,j) bottom (k=0) -> V(i,j-1, any k). So exiting H downward requires k=0.
- H(i,j) top (k=K-1) -> V(i,j+1, any k). Exiting H upward requires k=K-1.
- V(i,j) bottom -> H(i,j-1, K-1). Exiting V downward lands at K-1.
- V(i,j) top -> H(i,j+1, 0). Exiting V upward lands at 0.

Left/right:
- H(i,j) left -> V(i-1,j, K-1). Exiting H left lands at K-1.
- H(i,j) right -> V(i+1,j, 0). Exiting H right lands at 0.
- V(i,j) left (k=0) -> H(i-1,j, any k). Exiting V left requires k=0.
- V(i,j) right (k=K-1) -> H(i+1,j, any k). Exiting V right requires k=K-1.

So there is asymmetry. Let me summarize the "cost" to move between cells:

If we think of each cell as having a "type" and we need to pay some cost to traverse:

From H to V:
- Right: H -> V (cost 1, land at V.k=0)
- Left: H -> V (cost 1, land at V.k=K-1)
- Up: H -> V (cost 1, need H.k=K-1 first, land at V any k)
- Down: H -> V (cost 1, need H.k=0 first, land at V any k)

From V to H:
- Right: V -> H (cost 1, need V.k=K-1 first, land at H any k)
- Left: V -> H (cost 1, need V.k=0 first, land at H any k)
- Up: V -> H (cost 1, land at H.k=0)
- Down: V -> H (cost 1, land at H.k=K-1)

[tool call: python]
{
 "code": "from collections import deque\n\ndef min_tile_moves_bfs(K, S_x, S_y, T_x, T_y):\n    \"\"\"BFS to find min moves (for small coordinates only)\"\"\"\n    def get_tile(sx, sy):\n        x = sx + 0.5\n        y = sy + 0.5\n        i = int(x // K)\n        j = int(y // K)\n        if (i + j) % 2 == 0:\n            orient = 'H'\n            k = int(y - j*K)\n        else:\n            orient = 'V'\n            k = int(x - i*K)\n        return (i, j, k, orient)\n    \n    src = get_tile(S_x, S_y)\n    dst = get_tile(T_x, T_y)\n    \n    # For BFS, we need to generate neighbors\n    # But the state space is infinite. We'll limit search.\n    # For small examples, we can use a heuristic bound.\n    \n    from heapq import heappush, heappop\n    \n    # Let's use a simple BFS with limited range\n    visited = set()\n    q = deque()\n    q.append((src, 0))\n    visited.add(src)\n    \n    while q:\n        tile, dist = q.popleft()\n        i, j, k, o = tile\n        \n        if tile == dst:\n            return dist\n        \n        # Generate neighbors\n        if o == 'H':\n            # same cell: k+1, k-1\n            if k + 1 < K:\n                nb = (i, j, k+1, 'H')\n                if nb not in visited:\n                    visited.add(nb)\n                    q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            if k - 1 >= 0:\n                nb = (i, j, k-1, 'H')\n                if nb not in visited:\n                    visited.add(nb)\n                    q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # right -> V(i+1, j, 0)\n            nb = (i+1, j, 0, 'V')\n            if nb not in visited:\n                visited.add(nb)\n                q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # left -> V(i-1, j, K-1)\n            nb = (i-1, j, K-1, 'V')\n            if nb not in visited:\n                visited.add(nb)\n                q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # up -> V(i, j+1, any k) but only if k == K-1\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nb = (i, j+1, kk, 'V')\n                    if nb not in visited:\n                        visited.add(nb)\n                        q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # down -> V(i, j-1, any k) but only if k == 0\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nb = (i, j-1, kk, 'V')\n                    if nb not in visited:\n                        visited.add(nb)\n                        q.append((nb, dist+1))\n        else:  # V\n            # same cell: k+1, k-1\n            if k + 1 < K:\n                nb = (i, j, k+1, 'V')\n                if nb not in visited:\n                    visited.add(nb)\n                    q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            if k - 1 >= 0:\n                nb = (i, j, k-1, 'V')\n                if nb not in visited:\n                    visited.add(nb)\n                    q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # up -> H(i, j+1, 0)\n            nb = (i, j+1, 0, 'H')\n            if nb not in visited:\n                visited.add(nb)\n                q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # down -> H(i, j-1, K-1)\n            nb = (i, j-1, K-1, 'H')\n            if nb not in visited:\n                visited.add(nb)\n                q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # left -> H(i-1, j, any k) if k == 0\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nb = (i-1, j, kk, 'H')\n                    if nb not in visited:\n                        visited.add(nb)\n                        q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # right -> H(i+1, j, any k) if k == K-1\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nb = (i+1, j, kk, 'H')\n                    if nb not in visited:\n                        visited.add(nb)\n                        q.append((nb, dist+1))\n    \n    return None\n\n# Test with K=3\nprint(\"K=3, (-2,1) -> (4,-1):\", min_tile_moves_bfs(3, -2, 1, 4, -1))"
}

j*K)\n        else:\n            orient = 'V'\n            k = int(x - i*K)\n        return (i, j, k, orient)\n    \n    src = get_tile(S_x, S_y)\n    dst = get_tile(T_x, T_y)\n    \n    visited = set()\n    q = deque()\n    q.append((src, 0))\n    visited.add(src)\n    \n    while q:\n        tile, dist = q.popleft()\n        i, j, k, o = tile\n        \n        if tile == dst:\n            return dist\n        \n        # Generate neighbors\n        if o == 'H':\n            # same cell: k+1, k-1\n            if k + 1 < K:\n                nb = (i, j, k+1, 'H')\n                if nb not in visited:\n                    visited.add(nb)\n                    q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            if k - 1 >= 0:\n                nb = (i, j, k-1, 'H')\n                if nb not in visited:\n                    visited.add(nb)\n                    q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # right -> V(i+1, j, 0)\n            nb = (i+1, j, 0, 'V')\n            if nb not in visited:\n                visited.add(nb)\n                q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # left -> V(i-1, j, K-1)\n            nb = (i-1, j, K-1, 'V')\n            if nb not in visited:\n                visited.add(nb)\n                q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # up -> V(i, j+1, any k) but only if k == K-1\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nb = (i, j+1, kk, 'V')\n                    if nb not in visited:\n                        visited.add(nb)\n                        q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # down -> V(i, j-1, any k) but only if k == 0\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nb = (i, j-1, kk, 'V')\n                    if nb not in visited:\n                        visited.add(nb)\n                        q.append((nb, dist+1))\n        else:  # V\n            # same cell: k+1, k-1\n            if k + 1 < K:\n                nb = (i, j, k+1, 'V')\n                if nb not in visited:\n                    visited.add(nb)\n                    q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            if k - 1 >= 0:\n                nb = (i, j, k-1, 'V')\n                if nb not in visited:\n                    visited.add(nb)\n                    q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # up -> H(i, j+1, 0)\n            nb = (i, j+1, 0, 'H')\n            if nb not in visited:\n                visited.add(nb)\n                q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # down -> H(i, j-1, K-1)\n            nb = (i, j-1, K-1, 'H')\n            if nb not in visited:\n                visited.add(nb)\n                q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # left -> H(i-1, j, any k) if k == 0\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nb = (i-1, j, kk, 'H')\n                    if nb not in visited:\n                        visited.add(nb)\n                        q.append((nb, dist+1))\n            # right -> H(i+1, j, any k) if k == K-1\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nb = (i+1, j, kk, 'H')\n                    if nb not in visited:\n                        visited.add(nb)\n                        q.append((nb, dist+1))\n    \n    return None\n\n# Test the provided examples with BFS\ntests = [\n    (3, -2, 1, 4, -1, 4),\n    (4, 8, 8, 0, 2, 4),\n    (51, 94, 7, -90, -34, 8),\n    (67, 24, 3, 100, -23, 4),\n    (63, -9, 49, -45, 29, 2),\n    (19, -28, -65, 93, -76, 12),\n    (81, -36, 36, 80, 54, 3),\n    (20, -21, -75, 86, -82, 16),\n    (89, -16, 20, 43, -75, 4),\n    (47, 11, -20, 56, 63, 5),\n    (28, 41, 22, 13, 33, 4),\n    (35, -85, 40, -97, -77, 10),\n]\n\nfor K, sx, sy, tx, ty, expected in tests:\n    if abs(sx) < 200 and abs(sy) < 200 and abs(tx) < 200 and abs(ty) < 200 and K < 100:\n        result = min_tile_moves_bfs(K, sx, sy, tx, ty)\n        print(f\"K={K}, ({sx},{sy}) -> ({tx},{ty}): BFS={result}, expected={expected}, {'OK' if result == expected else 'FAIL'}\")"

Let me re-read the original problem:
- For integer triple (i, j, k) with 0 <= k < K:
  - If i and j have same parity: tile covers iK <= x <= (i+1)K and jK+k <= y <= jK+k+1
  - If i and j have different parity: tile covers iK+k <= x <= iK+k+1 and jK <= y <= (j+1)K

Two tiles are adjacent if their edges share a positive-length segment.

Let me carefully analyze the adjacency between two specific tiles.

Let's consider a horizontal tile (i, j, k) where i+j even:
- It covers [iK, (i+1)K] × [jK+k, jK+k+1]
- Its four edges:
  - Left: x = iK, y ∈ [jK+k, jK+k+1] (length 1)
  - Right: x = (i+1)K, y ∈ [jK+k, jK+k+1] (length 1)
  - Bottom: y = jK+k, x ∈ [iK, (i+1)K] (length K)
  - Top: y = jK+k+1, x ∈ [iK, (i+1)K] (length K)

Adjacent to what?

1. Within same cell (i,j), k differs by 1:
   - (i,j,k) bottom edge at y=jK+k touches (i,j,k-1) top edge at y=jK+(k-1)+1 = jK+k. The shared segment is x ∈ [iK, (i+1)K], length K. Adjacent! ✓

2. Left neighbor: cell (i-1, j). Since i+j even, (i-1)+j is odd. So (i-1, j) has vertical strips.
   - Vertical tile (i-1, j, k') covers [ (i-1)K + k', (i-1)K + k' + 1 ] × [jK, (j+1)K]
   - Its right edge: x = (i-1)K + k' + 1, y ∈ [jK, (j+1)K]
   - For this to touch the left edge of (i,j,k) at x=iK, we need (i-1)K + k' + 1 = iK → k' + 1 = K → k' = K-1.
   - So only vertical strip K-1 of (i-1, j) touches the left edge. The shared segment is y ∈ [jK+k, jK+k+1], length 1.
   - So (i,j,k) is adjacent to (i-1, j, K-1). ✓

3. Right neighbor: cell (i+1, j). (i+1)+j odd → vertical strips.
   - Vertical tile (i+1, j, k') left edge: x = (i+1)K + k', y ∈ [jK, (j+1)K]
   - Need (i+1)K + k' = (i+1)K → k' = 0.
   - So (i,j,k) adjacent to (i+1, j, 0). ✓

4. Bottom neighbor: cell (i, j-1). i+(j-1) odd → vertical strips.
   - Horizontal tile's bottom edge: y = jK+k, x ∈ [iK, (i+1)K]
   - Vertical tile (i, j-1, k') top edge: y = (j-1)K + K = jK, x ∈ [iK + k', iK + k' + 1]
   - Wait, the vertical tile covers y ∈ [(j-1)K, jK]. So its top edge is at y = jK, x ∈ [iK + k', iK + k' + 1].
   - For this to touch the bottom edge of horizontal tile at y=jK+k, we need jK+k = jK → k = 0.
   - So only when k=0 does the horizontal tile touch the cell below. The shared segment is x ∈ [iK + k', iK + k' + 1] ∩ [iK, (i+1)K] = [iK + k', iK + k' + 1], length 1.
   - So (i,j,0) is adjacent to (i, j-1, k') for ALL k' ∈ [0, K-1]. ✓

5. Top neighbor: cell (i, j+1). i+(j+1) odd → vertical strips.
   - Horizontal tile's top edge: y = jK+k+1, x ∈ [iK, (i+1)K]
   - Vertical tile (i, j+1, k') bottom edge: y = (j+1)K, x ∈ [iK + k', iK + k' + 1]
   - Need jK+k+1 = (j+1)K → k+1 = K → k = K-1.
   - So (i,j,K-1) adjacent to (i, j+1, k') for ALL k'. ✓

Now for vertical tiles (i, j, k) where i+j odd:
- Covers [iK + k, iK + k + 1] × [jK, (j+1)K]
- Edges:

  - Right: x = iK+k+1, y ∈ [jK, (j+1)K] (length K)

  - Top: y = (j+1)K, x ∈ [iK+k, iK+k+1] (length 1)

1. Within cell: (i,j,k) left/right adjacent to (i,j,k±1). Left edge at x=iK+k touches right edge of k-1 at x=iK+(k-1)+1=iK+k. Length K. ✓

2. Left neighbor: cell (i-1, j). (i-1)+j even → horizontal strips.

 "code": "from collections import deque\n\ndef bfs_distance(K, S_x, S_y, T_x, T_y):\n    def get_tile(sx, sy):\n        x = sx + 0.5\n        y = sy + 0.5\n        i = int(x // K)\n        j = int(y // K)\n        if (i + j) % 2 == 0:\n            orient = 'H'\n            k = int(y - j*K)\n        else:\n            orient = 'V'\n            k = int(x - i*K)\n        return (i, j, k, orient)\n    \n    src = get_tile(S_x, S_y)\n    dst = get_tile(T_x, T_y)\n    if src == dst:\n        return 0\n    visited = {src: 0}\n    q = deque([src])\n    while q:\n        tile = q.popleft()\n        i, j, k, o = tile\n        d = visited[tile]\n        if o == 'H':\n            nbs = []\n            if k + 1 < K: nbs.append((i, j, k+1, 'H'))\n            if k - 1 >= 0: nbs.append((i, j, k-1, 'H'))\n            nbs.append((i+1, j, 0, 'V'))\n            nbs.append((i-1, j, K-1, 'V'))\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K): nbs.append((i, j+1, kk, 'V'))\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K): nbs.append((i, j-1, kk, 'V'))\n        else:\n            nbs = []\n            if k + 1 < K: nbs.append((i, j, k+1, 'V'))\n            if k - 1 >= 0: nbs.append((i, j, k-1, 'V'))\n            nbs.append((i, j+1, 0, 'H'))\n            nbs.append((i, j-1, K-1, 'H'))\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K): nbs.append((i-1, j, kk, 'H'))\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K): nbs.append((i+1, j, kk, 'H'))\n        for nb in nbs:\n            if nb not in visited:\n                if nb == dst:\n                    return d + 1\n                visited[nb] = d + 1\n                q.append(nb)\n    return None\n\n# Compute my BFS for all cases that fit in reasonable bounds\ntests = [\n    (3, -2, 1, 4, -1, 4),\n    (4, 8, 8, 0, 2, 4),\n    (51, 94, 7, -90, -34, 8),\n    (67, 24, 3, 100, -23, 4),\n    (63, -9, 49, -45, 29, 2),\n    (19, -28, -65, 93, -76, 12),\n    (81, -36, 36, 80, 54, 3),\n    (20, -21, -75, 86, -82, 16),\n    (89, -16, 20, 43, -75, 4),\n    (47, 11, -20, 56, 63, 5),\n    (28, 41, 22, 13, 33, 4),\n    (35, -85, 40, -97, -77, 10),\n    (2, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0),\n    (2, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0),\n    (2, 0, 0, 0, 1, 1),\n    (3, 1, -1, 2, -2, 1),\n]\n\nprint(\"Comparing BFS vs expected:\")\nfor K, sx, sy, tx, ty, expected in tests:\n    if abs(sx) < 200 and abs(sy) < 200 and abs(tx) < 200 and abs(ty) < 200 and K < 200:\n        result = bfs_distance(K, sx, sy, tx, ty)\n        status = \"OK\" if result == expected else f\"DIFF (BFS={result}, exp={expected})\"\n        print(f\"  K={K}: {status}\")"
}

 "code": "from collections import deque\n\ndef bfs_distance(K, S_x, S_y, T_x, T_y):\n    def get_tile(sx, sy):\n        x = sx + 0.5\n        y = sy + 0.5\n        i = int(x // K)\n        j = int(y // K)\n        if (i + j) % 2 == 0:\n            orient = 'H'\n            k = int(y - j*K)\n        else:\n            orient = 'V'\n            k = int(x - i*K)\n        return (i, j, k, orient)\n    \n    src = get_tile(S_x, S_y)\n    dst = get_tile(T_x, T_y)\n    if src == dst:\n        return 0\n    visited = {src: 0}\n    q = deque([src])\n    while q:\n        tile = q.popleft()\n        i, j, k, o = tile\n        d = visited[tile]\n        if o == 'H':\n            nbs = []\n            if k + 1 < K:\n                nbs.append((i, j, k+1, 'H'))\n            if k - 1 >= 0:\n                nbs.append((i, j, k-1, 'H'))\n            nbs.append((i+1, j, 0, 'V'))\n            nbs.append((i-1, j, K-1, 'V'))\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nbs.append((i, j+1, kk, 'V'))\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nbs.append((i, j-1, kk, 'V'))\n        else:\n            nbs = []\n            if k + 1 < K:\n                nbs.append((i, j, k+1, 'V'))\n            if k - 1 >= 0:\n                nbs.append((i, j, k-1, 'V'))\n            nbs.append((i, j+1, 0, 'H'))\n            nbs.append((i, j-1, K-1, 'H'))\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nbs.append((i-1, j, kk, 'H'))\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K):\n                    nbs.append((i+1, j, kk, 'H'))\n        for nb in nbs:\n            if nb not in visited:\n                if nb == dst:\n                    return d + 1\n                visited[nb] = d + 1\n                q.append(nb)\n    return None\n\n# Compute distance between cells (i1,j1) and (i2,j2) for various k values\ndef cell_dist(K, i1, j1, k1, o1, i2, j2, k2, o2):\n    \"\"\"Find a point in the source tile and target tile to use BFS.\"\"\"\n    # We need integer coordinates that map to these tiles\n    # For H(i,j,k): any point with x in (iK, (i+1)K) and y = jK + k + 0.5\n    # For V(i,j,k): x = iK + k + 0.5, y in (jK, (j+1)K)\n    if o1 == 'H':\n        sx = i1 * K + K // 2  # middle of x range\n        sy = j1 * K + k1\n    else:\n        sx = i1 * K + k1\n        sy = j1 * K + K // 2\n    \n    if o2 == 'H':\n        tx = i2 * K + K // 2\n        ty = j2 * K + k2\n    else:\n        tx = i2 * K + k2\n        ty = j2 * K + K // 2\n    \n    return bfs_distance(K, sx, sy, tx, ty)\n\n# Let me test some specific patterns\n# Distance between H(i,j,k) and H(i+1,j, anything)?\nprint(\"K=5:\")\nfor k1 in range(5):\n    for k2 in range(5):\n        d = cell_dist(5, 0, 0, k1, 'H', 1, 0, k2, 'H')\n        print(f\"  H(0,0,{k1}) -> H(1,0,{k2}): {d}\")\n\nprint(\"\\nK=5:\")\nfor k1 in range(5):\n    for k2 in range(5):\n        d = cell_dist(5, 0, 0, k1, 'H', 0, 1, k2, 'H')\n        print(f\"  H(0,0,{k1}) -> H(0,1,{k2}): {d}\")"
}

Actually, more generally: cells of type H have i+j even. Cells of type V have i+j odd. So moving from one cell to an adjacent cell always flips the type. Moving two steps in any direction returns to the same type (H->V->H or V->H->V).

So the distance between same-type cells involves an even number of cell-to-cell moves.

[tool call: python]
{
 "code": "# Let me design a distance function based on the macro model and test it\n\nfrom collections import deque\n\ndef bfs_distance(K, S_x, S_y, T_x, T_y):\n    def get_tile(sx, sy):\n        x = sx + 0.5\n        y = sy + 0.5\n        i = int(x // K)\n        j = int(y // K)\n        if (i + j) % 2 == 0:\n            orient = 'H'\n            k = int(y - j*K)\n        else:\n            orient = 'V'\n            k = int(x - i*K)\n        return (i, j, k, orient)\n    \n    src = get_tile(S_x, S_y)\n    dst = get_tile(T_x, T_y)\n    if src == dst:\n        return 0\n    visited = {src: 0}\n    q = deque([src])\n    while q:\n        tile = q.popleft()\n        i, j, k, o = tile\n        d = visited[tile]\n        if o == 'H':\n            nbs = []\n            if k + 1 < K: nbs.append((i, j, k+1, 'H'))\n            if k - 1 >= 0: nbs.append((i, j, k-1, 'H'))\n            nbs.append((i+1, j, 0, 'V'))\n            nbs.append((i-1, j, K-1, 'V'))\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K): nbs.append((i, j+1, kk, 'V'))\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K): nbs.append((i, j-1, kk, 'V'))\n        else:\n            nbs = []\n            if k + 1 < K: nbs.append((i, j, k+1, 'V'))\n            if k - 1 >= 0: nbs.append((i, j, k-1, 'V'))\n            nbs.append((i, j+1, 0, 'H'))\n            nbs.append((i, j-1, K-1, 'H'))\n            if k == 0:\n                for kk in range(K): nbs.append((i-1, j, kk, 'H'))\n            if k == K-1:\n                for kk in range(K): nbs.append((i+1, j, kk, 'H'))\n        for nb in nbs:\n            if nb not in visited:\n                if nb == dst:\n                    return d + 1\n                visited[nb] = d + 1\n                q.append(nb)\n    return None\n\n# Test compilation\nprint(\"OK\")"
}

 "code": "# Check if point belongs to exactly one tile\ndef count_tiles_containing(K, x, y):\n    \"\"\"Count how many tiles contain the point (x, y).\"\"\"\n    count = 0\n    tiles = []\n    # We need to search over i, j, k\n    # Bound the search\n    i_min = int(x // K) - 2\n    i_max = int(x // K) + 2\n    j_min = int(y // K) - 2\n    j_max = int(y // K) + 2\n    for i in range(i_min, i_max + 1):\n        for j in range(j_min, j_max + 1):\n            for k in range(K):\n                if (i + j) % 2 == 0:  # H\n                    x0, x1 = i*K, (i+1)*K\n                    y0, y1 = j*K + k, j*K + k + 1\n                else:  # V\n                    x0, x1 = i*K + k, i*K + k + 1\n                    y0, y1 = j*K, (j+1)*K\n                if x0 <= x <= x1 and y0 <= y <= y1:\n                    count += 1\n                    tiles.append((i, j, k, 'H' if (i+j)%2==0 else 'V'))\n    return count, tiles\n\n# Test with some points\nfor K in [2, 3, 5]:\n    for x, y in [(0.5, 0.5), (1.5, 0.5), (0.5, 1.5), (2.5, 2.5), (-0.5, -0.5), (K-0.5, K-0.5), (K+0.5, K+0.5)]:\n        cnt, tiles = count_tiles_containing(K, x, y)\n        if cnt != 1:\n            print(f\"K={K}, ({x},{y}): count={cnt}, tiles={tiles}\")"
